-lc \\\"sed -n '1,260p' repro/exp_001_bias_bound_mvp/run_experiment.py\\\"\",\"aggregated_output\":\"\\\"\\\"\\\"Run the exp_001 EC-HTE bias-bound MVP simulation.\\n\\nThis script builds a semi-synthetic randomized-treatment DGP where the true\\neffect modifier G is observed through a noisy label Z. It compares uncorrected\\nGATE estimates against pooled/global and subgroup-specific confusion correction.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport math\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import Iterable\\n\\nimport matplotlib\\n\\nmatplotlib.use(\\\"Agg\\\")\\n\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport seaborn as sns\\n\\n\\nMETHOD_UNCORRECTED = \\\"uncorrected_z_direct\\\"\\nMETHOD_GLOBAL_ORACLE = \\\"global_corrected_pooled_oracle\\\"\\nMETHOD_STRATIFIED_ORACLE = \\\"stratified_corrected_oracle\\\"\\nMETHOD_GLOBAL_B = \\\"global_corrected_estimated_B\\\"\\nMETHOD_STRATIFIED_B = \\\"stratified_corrected_estimated_B\\\"\\n\\n\\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass DGPConfig:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Parameters defining one semi-synthetic DGP configuration.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    name: str\\n    tau_values: np.ndarray\\n    error_rates: np.ndarray\\n    group_probs: np.ndarray\\n    noise_sd: float = 1.0\\n\\n\\ndef parse_float_list(value: str) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Parse comma-separated floats into a numpy array.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    return np.array([float(part.strip()) for part in value.split(\\\",\\\") if part.strip()])\\n\\n\\ndef parse_int_list(value: str) -> list[int]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Parse comma-separated integers into a list.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    return [int(part.strip()) for part in value.split(\\\",\\\") if part.strip()]\\n\\n\\ndef default_config(direction: str, k: int) -> DGPConfig:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Return tuned k=3/4 DGPs with positive or negative error-CATE correlation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    if k == 4:\\n        tau_values = np.array([0.15, 0.85, 1.02, 1.05])\\n        positive_errors = np.array([0.01, 0.06, 0.13, 0.46])\\n        group_probs = np.array([0.556, 0.102, 0.089, 0.253])\\n    elif k == 3:\\n        tau_values = np.array([0.10, 0.65, 1.05])\\n        positive_errors = np.array([0.01, 0.08, 0.42])\\n        group_probs = np.array([0.58, 0.14, 0.28])\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"This MVP supports k=3 or k=4.\\\")\\n\\n    if direction == \\\"positive\\\":\\n        error_rates = positive_errors\\n    elif direction == \\\"negative\\\":\\n        error_rates = positive_errors[::-1]\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"direction must be positive or negative\\\")\\n\\n    return DGPConfig(\\n        name=f\\\"{direction}_correlation\\\",\\n        tau_values=tau_values,\\n        error_rates=error_rates,\\n        group_probs=group_probs / group_probs.sum(),\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef make_confusion_matrix(error_rates: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Create P(Z=z | G=g) with subgroup-specific uniform off-diagonal errors.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    k = len(error_rates)\\n    if np.any(error_rates < 0) or np.any(error_rates >= (k - 1) / k):\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"error rates must be nonnegative and below the invertibility limit\\\")\\n\\n    confusion = np.zeros((k, k), dtype=float)\\n    for g, error_rate in enumerate(error_rates):\\n        confusion[g, :] = error_rate / (k - 1)\\n        confusion[g, g] = 1.0 - error_rate\\n    return confusion\\n\\n\\ndef posterior_true_given_noisy(confusion: np.ndarray, group_probs: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute W[z, g] = P(G=g | Z=z) from P(Z|G) and group probabilities.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    noisy_probs = group_probs @ confusion\\n    if np.any(noisy_probs <= 0):\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"invalid noisy-label probabilities\\\")\\n    return np.array(\\n        [\\n            [group_probs[g] * confusion[g, z] / noisy_probs[z] for g in range(len(group_probs))]\\n            for z in range(len(group_probs))\\n        ],\\n        dtype=float,\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef global_confusion_from_error(error_rate: float, k: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build a pooled symmetric confusion matrix with one global error rate.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    clipped = float(np.clip(error_rate, 1e-6, (k - 1) / k - 1e-6))\\n    confusion = np.full((k, k), clipped / (k - 1), dtype=float)\\n    np.fill_diagonal(confusion, 1.0 - clipped)\\n    return confusion\\n\\n\\ndef simulate_dataset(config: DGPConfig, n: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Generate randomized treatment data with true G and noisy modifier Z.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    k = len(config.tau_values)\\n    true_g = rng.choice(k, size=n, p=config.group_probs)\\n    treatment = rng.binomial(1, 0.5, size=n)\\n    base = 0.2 * np.sin(true_g)\\n    outcome = base + config.tau_values[true_g] * treatment + rng.normal(0.0, config.noise_sd, size=n)\\n\\n    confusion = make_confusion_matrix(config.error_rates)\\n    noisy_z = np.empty(n, dtype=int)\\n    for g in range(k):\\n        mask = true_g == g\\n        noisy_z[mask] = rng.choice(k, size=int(mask.sum()), p=confusion[g])\\n\\n    return {\\\"G\\\": true_g, \\\"Z\\\": noisy_z, \\\"T\\\": treatment, \\\"Y\\\": outcome}\\n\\n\\ndef gate_by_label(labels: np.ndarray, treatment: np.ndarray, outcome: np.ndarray, k: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Estimate subgroup treatment effects by difference in means for each label.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    estimates = np.full(k, np.nan, dtype=float)\\n    for label in range(k):\\n        mask = labels == label\\n        treated = mask & (treatment == 1)\\n        control = mask & (treatment == 0)\\n        if treated.sum() == 0 or control.sum() == 0:\\n            continue\\n        estimates[label] = outcome[treated].mean() - outcome[control].mean()\\n\\n    if np.any(np.isnan(estimates)):\\n        fill_value = np.nanmean(estimates)\\n        estimates = np.nan_to_num(estimates, nan=fill_value)\\n    return estimates\\n\\n\\ndef corrected_gate(observed_gate_z: np.ndarray, posterior_w: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Invert tau_Z = W tau_G using a stable pseudo-inverse.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    return np.linalg.pinv(posterior_w, rcond=1e-8) @ observed_gate_z\\n\\n\\ndef estimate_confusion_from_expert_sample(\\n    true_g: np.ndarray,\\n    noisy_z: np.ndarray,\\n    k: int,\\n    budget_b: int,\\n    rng: np.random.Generator,\\n    alpha: float = 1.0,\\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Estimate subgroup-specific and pooled confusion from a finite expert sample.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    sample_size = min(int(budget_b), len(true_g))\\n    idx = rng.choice(len(true_g), size=sample_size, replace=False)\\n    sampled_g = true_g[idx]\\n    sampled_z = noisy_z[idx]\\n\\n    joint_counts = np.full((k, k), alpha, dtype=float)\\n    for g, z in zip(sampled_g, sampled_z, strict=False):\\n        joint_counts[g, z] += 1.0\\n\\n    confusion = joint_counts / joint_counts.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n    group_counts = np.bincount(sampled_g, minlength=k).astype(float) + alpha\\n    group_probs = group_counts / group_counts.sum()\\n    pooled_error = float(np.mean(sampled_g != sampled_z))\\n    pooled_error = float(np.clip(pooled_error, 1e-6, (k - 1) / k - 1e-6))\\n    return confusion, group_probs, pooled_error\\n\\n\\ndef diagnostic_product(error_rates: np.ndarray, tau_values: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute the bias-bound diagnostic: error heterogeneity times CATE heterogeneity.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    return float(np.std(error_rates, ddof=0) * np.std(tau_values, ddof=0))\\n\\n\\ndef run_replication(\\n    config: DGPConfig,\\n    n: int,\\n    replication: int,\\n    seed: int,\\n    budget_b: int,\\n    include_estimated_budget: bool,\\n) -> list[dict[str, float | int | str]]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run one replication and return method/group-level result rows.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 100_000 * replication + 17 * n)\\n    k = len(config.tau_values)\\n    data = simulate_dataset(config, n, rng)\\n    observed_gate_z = gate_by_label(data[\\\"Z\\\"], data[\\\"T\\\"], data[\\\"Y\\\"], k)\\n\\n    true_confusion = make_confusion_matrix(config.error_rates)\\n    weighted_error = float(np.dot(config.group_probs, config.error_rates))\\n    global_confusion = global_confusion_from_error(weighted_error, k)\\n\\n    estimates: dict[str, np.ndarray] = {\\n        METHOD_UNCORRECTED: observed_gate_z,\\n        METHOD_GLOBAL_ORACLE: corrected_gate(\\n            observed_gate_z, posterior_true_given_noisy(global_confusion, config.group_probs)\\n        ),\\n        METHOD_STRATIFIED_ORACLE: corrected_gate(\\n            observed_gate_z, posterior_true_given_noisy(true_confusion, config.group_probs)\\n        ),\\n    }\\n\\n    if include_estimated_budget and budget_b > 0:\\n        est_confusion, est_probs, est_pooled_error = estimate_confusion_from_expert_sample(\\n            data[\\\"G\\\"], data[\\\"Z\\\"], k, budget_b, rng\\n        )\\n        est_global = global_confusion_from_error(est_pooled_error, k)\\n        estimates[METHOD_GLOBAL_B] = corrected_gate(\\n            observed_gate_z, posterior_true_given_noisy(est_global, est_probs)\\n        )\\n        estimates[METHOD_STRATIFIED_B] = corrected_gate(\\n            observed_gate_z, posterior_true_given_noisy(est_confusion, est_probs)\\n        )\\n\\n    rows: list[dict[str, float | int | str]] = []\\n    diag = diagnostic_product(config.error_rates, config.tau_values)\\n    corr = float(np.corrcoef(config.error_rates, np.abs(config.tau_values))[0, 1])\\n    for method, gate_hat in estimates.items():\\n        for group in range(k):\\n            estimate = float(gate_hat[group])\\n            truth = float(config.tau_values[group])\\n            rows.append(\\n                {\\n                    \\\"config\\\": config.name,\\n                    \\\"n\\\": n,\\n                    \\\"replication\\\": replication,\\n                    \\\"method\\\": method,\\n                    \\\"group\\\": group,\\n                    \\\"estimate\\\": estimate,\\n                    \\\"true_tau\\\": truth,\\n                    \\\"bias\\\": estimate - truth,\\n                    \\\"abs_bias\\\": abs(estimate - truth),\\n                    \\\"squared_error\\\": (estimate - truth) ** 2,\\n                    \\\"error_rate\\\": float(config.error_rates[group]),\\n                    \\\"diagnostic_product\\\": \\\"first\\\"),\\n        )\\n        .sort_values([\\\"config\\\", \\\"n\\\", \\\"method\\\"])\\n    )\\n\\n    ratio_rows = []\\n    for (config_name, n), frame in summary.groupby([\\\"config\\\", \\\"n\\\"]):\\n        uncorrected = frame.loc[frame[\\\"method\\\"] == METHOD_UNCORRECTED, \\\"mean_abs_bias\\\"]\\n        if uncorrected.empty:\\n            continue\\n        base = float(uncorrected.iloc[0])\\n        for _, row in frame.iterrows():\\n            ratio_rows.append(\\n                {\\n                    \\\"config\\\": config_name,\\n                    \\\"n\\\": int(n),\\n                    \\\"method\\\": row[\\\"method\\\"],\\n                    \\\"bias_ratio_vs_uncorrected\\\": float(row[\\\"mean_abs_bias\\\"] / base) if base else np.nan,\\n                }\\n            )\\n    ratios = pd.DataFrame(ratio_rows)\\n    summary = summary.merge(ratios, on=[\\\"config\\\", \\\"n\\\", \\\"method\\\"], how=\\\"left\\\")\\n\\n    key_metrics: dict[str, object] = {\\\"by_config_n_method\\\": summary.to_dict(orient=\\\"records\\\")}\\n    for config_name in sorted(summary[\\\"config\\\"].unique()):\\n        subset = summary[(summary[\\\"config\\\"] == config_name) & (summary[\\\"method\\\"] == METHOD_GLOBAL_ORACLE)]\\n        key_metrics[f\\\"{config_name}_global_vs_uncorrected_bias_ratio_mean\\\"] = float(\\n            subset[\\\"bias_ratio_vs_uncorrected\\\"].mean()\\n        )\\n    return summary, key_metrics\\n\\n\\ndef write_plots(summary: pd.DataFrame, out_dir: Path) -> Path:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Write a x=\\\"diagnostic_product\\\",\\n        y=\\\"bias_ratio_vs_uncorrected\\\",\\n        hue=\\\"config\\\",\\n        style=\\\"method\\\",\\n        size=\\\"n\\\",\\n        sizes=(80, 180),\\n    )\\n    plt.axhline(1.0, color=\\\"black\\\", linewidth=1.0, linestyle=\\\"--\\\")\\n    plt.xlabel(\\\"misclassification heterogeneity x CATE heterogeneity\\\")\\n    plt.ylabel(\\\"mean absolute bias ratio vs uncorrected\\\")\\n    plt.tight_layout()\\n    plt.savefig(plot_path, dpi=180)\\n    plt.close()\\n    return plot_path\\n\\n\\ndef write_report(summary: pd.DataFrame, metrics: dict[str, object], out_dir: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> Path:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Write a concise markdown report for the experiment artifact.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    report_path = out_dir / \\\"report.md\\\"\\n    positive_ratio = metrics.get(\\\"positive_correlation_global_vs_uncorrected_bias_ratio_mean\\\")\\n    negative_ratio = METHOD_STRATIFIED_ORACLE])]\\n\\n    lines = [\\n        \\\"# exp_001_bias_bound_mvp\\\",\\n        \\\"\\\",\\n        \\\"CPU-only semi-synthetic GATE experiment with oracle-known and B-sample pseudo-inverse matrix inversion; pooled error rates are clipped to the legal invertibility range.\\\",\\n        \\\"\\\",\\n        \\\"## Core summary\\\",\\n        \\\"\\\",\\n It supports a differential-misclassification feasibility study if anonymized transcripts and record-level expert/LLM labels are obtained from the authors or World Bank. Public-only reproduction is not currently supported, because transcripts are not publicly downloadable and record-level expert labels, LLM labels, and split IDs were not found in the public repository.\n\n## Evidence Matrix\n\n| Field | Status | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Data availability | Partial | Article data availability states code is public at https://github.com/worldbank/iQual, interview transcripts cannot be public for privacy, and an anonymized version will be available on request. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| Schema | Partial | Paper reports 2,407 open-ended interviews, 32,463 QA pairs, baseline household/adult survey variables, and 19 main code labels from a coding tree. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| Text | Partial | Interviews were conducted in Bengali/Rohingya dialect, transcribed into Bengali, translated to English by Google Translate, and manually corrected for coding; raw text requires request due privacy. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| LLM annotation | Supported by method, not by public labels | Paper documents GPT-3.5-turbo, GPT-4o mini, Llama-2 13B, and Llama-3 8B annotation with code-specific few-shot and chain-of-thought prompts; record-level labels were not located in the GitHub tree. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246; repo: https://github.com/worldbank/iQual. |\n| Expert labels | Partial | 789 transcripts were human-coded in Atlas.ti by two annotators with reviewer resolution, from 400 R2 and 400 R3 sampled interviews minus 11 unannotated cases; public record-level labels were not found. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| Subgroup variables | Supported in paper; data linkage pending | Required variables are explicitly used: refugee status, eldest child's gender, and parent's years of education. Quantitative table also reports refugee status, female eldest child, female household head, parent education, and related variables. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| Split | Partial / 待核验 | Paper reports R2/R3 sampling, an 80/20 train/test split and 3-fold CV for iQual modeling, but public split IDs for expert/LLM validation were not found. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246. |\n| License / IRB | Partial / 待核验 | iQual code license is MIT: https://github.com/worldbank/iQual/blob/main/LICENSE.md. Public CBPS baseline Dataverse metadata reports CC0 1.0 for baseline survey DOI https://doi.org/10.7910/DVN/V614VB. Interview transcript license, data-use terms, and IRB/consent constraints remain 待核验. |\n\n## Feasibility For EC-HTE\n\n可支撑:\n- Differential LLM misclassification analysis by respondent characteristics, because the paper explicitly tests whether LLM prediction errors are associated with subject characteristics including refugee status, gender, and education. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246; World Bank summary: https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099348304162431121/pdf/IDU1271bc2541c5e8144f51b8cc1be231f985cc6.pdf.\n- Leave-expert-out or budget subsampling design in principle, because 789 expert-coded transcripts exist and the code labels are binary at QA/interview level. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246.\n\n部分可支撑:\n- Reproducing schema and subgroup covariates from public sources, because the CBPS baseline/HFT public data describe host/camp status and household/individual variables, but linkage to restricted transcripts and coded labels must be obtained. Yale CBPS page: https://macmillan.yale.edu/refugee/coxs-bazar-panel-survey; World Bank HFT DOI: https://doi.org/10.48529/2zfw-ht33.\n- LLM annotation reproduction, because prompts and method are described in the paper/supplement, but exact record-level LLM outputs are not in the public iQual repository as checked via GitHub API on 2026-05-31. Repo: https://github.com/worldbank/iQual.\n\n暂不可支撑:\n- Public-only real-data experiment with auditable unit-level confusion matrices, because raw/anonymized transcripts, expert labels, LLM labels, and split IDs are not currently public. Source: data availability statement in https://doi.org/10.1177/00491241251338246.\n- Any causal accuracy claim such as real-data PEHE/GATE RMSE, because the corpus supplies annotation labels and respondent covariates, not ground-truth treatment effects.     "aggregated_output": "./docs/PROJECT_BRIEF.md:9:This problem is urgent. LLM-as-annotator has become the default pipeline for computational social science (Audinet de Pieuchon et al., EMNLP 2025, benchmarking DSL vs. PPI debiasing; StratPPI, Fisch et al., NeurIPS 2024, improving PPI via stratification), and researchers increasingly use LLM annotations not just for ATE estimation (where DSL and PPI provide corrections) but for subgroup analysis and effect modification—yet no correction method exists for this latter use case. Messing (arXiv:2604.11581) documents that hidden measurement error in LLM pipelines causes naive confidence intervals to under-cover by 40–60%, but this analysis remains at the aggregate level. Ashwin et al. (Sociological Methods & Research, 2025) provide direct empirical evidence that LLM coding errors in refugee interview transcripts correlate systematically with respondents' refugee status, gender, and education level—precisely the variables used as effect modifiers in downstream causal analysis. Any study using LLM-annotated text variables as moderators produces unreliable heterogeneity findings whenever annotation error rates differ across subgroups—and Ashwin et al. show this is the norm, not the exception.\n",
    "exit_code": 0,
    "status": "completed"
  }
}
